<meta>Continuity: This page completes the sentence from the previous page, noting that the conclusions drawn from the Art would be "joyful" to a practitioner. It then transitions into discussing the first "Conclusion" regarding the validity of alchemy as a true science.</meta>

be right joyful to a Son of the Art <term>Son of the Art|A traditional term for an initiate or dedicated practitioner of alchemy, implying a spiritual or intellectual lineage</term>.

The first Conclusion <note>original: *Conclusion*</note> is merely intended as a preparation for the discovery of the Art, providing assurance that the same is true and real in fact. We do not wish to dwell extensively on this point here, especially since our intention is not to entice or invite anyone to the Art; rather, we only intend to guide and lead its sons in their diligent investigations and tireless searching or tracking.

We could prove and demonstrate the possibility of the Art through many arguments <note>original: *Argumenta*</note>, as has already been done sufficiently in our *Marrow of Alchemy* <note>original: *Medulla Alchimiæ*; a Latin title meaning "the marrow" or "the essence" of alchemy, a common title for George Ripley's works</note>. Likewise, in another treatise, the matter has been fully and philosophically <note>original: *Philosophisch*; in this context, "philosophical" refers to the study of natural laws and the hidden properties of matter</note> detailed and its truth proven. Therefore, our **Art** <note>The practice of alchemy, often called the "Sacred Art."</note> consists in this: that we join together two **metallic principles** <term>Metallic Principles|In the Paracelsian tradition, these are the fundamental components of all matter: Salt (body), Sulfur (soul), and Mercury (spirit).</term> or beginnings. <meta>This page continues the discussion of the "three Mercuries" or keys of the philosophers, following the previous page's assertion that there is ultimately only one matter and one process. The first is that by which we calcine <term>calcine|original: *calciniren*; to reduce a metal to a fine powder or "ash" through heat without melting it</term> the perfect bodies <note>Refers to gold and silver, the "perfect" or "noble" metals.</note>—to which no impure body is added—as the Green Lion <note>original: *grüne Löwe*; a cryptic term often referring to a potent acidic solvent, like oil of vitriol, or a specific preparation of antimony or lead</note>, which is brought about solely and uniquely from pure mineral sulfur, which is the only visible mercury at the beginning of our work.

But when, according to the teaching of Trevisanus <note>original: *Trevisani*; Bernard of Treviso (1406–1490), an Italian alchemist who famously spent his life and fortune seeking the Stone and wrote of the "Philosopher's Fountain."</note>, the water from this fountain has been drawn out until it has become truly retentive or self-holding; or when, according to the opinion of Ripley, the earth is dried out until it has become parched and thus the life is killed: then, by the command of the Most High, the vegetable soul appears in this corpse <note>original: *Cadavere* or dead body; the inert material remaining after its "spirit" has been extracted</note>. And then the Antimonial Vinegar <note>original: *Antimonialischer Essig*; a key solvent mentioned by the legendary alchemist Artephius, said to dissolve metals into their primary matter</note> of Artephius is visible, which is a mixture of the antimonial and mercurial spirits for the preparation of our May-dew <note>original: *Mayen-Thau*; alchemists believed that dew collected in the spring, especially in May, contained a "celestial nitre" or life-force from the stars that could fertilize the alchemical "earth."</note>, which can make the earth fruitful.

This circulates so long until the perfect body of the Sun <note>original: *Sonnen*; in alchemical symbolism, the Sun always represents Gold.</note> is opened through putrefaction <term>putrefaction|the stage of the "Great Work" where the matter turns black and decays, signaling the death of the old form before a new one can arise</term>, and then appears the

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the "Three Mercuries." The previous page ended abruptly with a mention of "Antimonial Vi—" (likely Vitriol or Vinegar); this page identifies the third and final Mercury as Azoth and details its role in the transformation of gold.</meta>

Azoth <term>Azoth|The "universal solvent" or "Mercury of the Philosophers." It was considered the essential agent of transformation, often symbolized by the Alpha and Omega to represent the beginning and the end of the Great Work.</term>, which is the third and entirely permanent moisture, of which it is said by the Philosophers: *Fire and Azoth are sufficient for you.* <note>original: *Ignis & Azoth tibi sufficiunt.*</note> That is, fire and Azoth are enough for you.

Therefore, when the Philosopher commands one to work with "likes with likes" (or in things that are of the same kind), this is his command alone: that he bids you, by means of the Antimonial-Saturnine Mercury <term>Antimonial-Saturnine Mercury|A specialized solvent derived from antimony and lead (Saturn), used to "wash" or purify metals without destroying their "seed."</term>, to whiten the red Laton <term>Laton|original: *Laton*; an alchemical term for an impure metallic body—often described as "foul gold"—that must be cleansed of its "redness" or impurities to reach a state of purity.</term> and to extract an incombustible water from the perfect body of Sol <note>original: *Cörper Solis*; the "Body of the Sun," a standard alchemical code for gold.</note>; afterwards to coagulate the solvent, and to coagulate the dissolved substance with an unbreakable or inseparable union.

Because it is then only spiritual gold that we seek—which is first white and then red—it behooves us, according to the teaching of Trevisanus <note>Bernard of Treviso (1406–1490), an Italian alchemist famous for his search for the "Philosopher's Fountain."</note>, to whiten our red Laton with a white, lukewarm, and tempered water; or, according to the opinion of Artephius <note>original: *Artephii*; Artephius (c. 1150), a legendary figure whose writings focused heavily on the use of "Antimonial Vinegar" as a key to the work.</note>, with the Antimonial-Saturnine Mercury, whose water must for this purpose be mercurial and white, so that it may be able to make the gold white and not burn it.

) 50 (

...[if the fire] is taken into account / it makes the **Work** easier; but where it is not taken into account / it makes all the applied labor of a man vain and lost. **purpose**, which is **perfection**. Therefore, and not without good reason, it is called the **fire against nature** <note>original: "Feuer wider die Natur." **regimen** <note>original: "regimen oder Regierung." In alchemy, the "regimen" refers to the specific stage of the process and the precise management of heat and time required for that stage.</note> or rule is maintained, upon which he must attend with the greatest possible care. Sublimation is the process where a substance is heated until it rises as a vapor and solidifies again at the top of the vessel. The author suggests this "spirit-fire" is so pervasive it does not need to rise or move.</note>, because it knows nothing of the laws of dimensions.

<page-num>159</page-num>

but is equal above and below and in all places. This is that of which **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundational texts of alchemy are attributed.</note> says, namely: That what is above is equal to that which is below, and thus also vice versa. It may well be compared with the **soul** in the body, which is located in every part of it at one time, and is bound or restricted to none.

This is the true **solution** <note>original: "solution oder Aufflösung."</note> or resolution of one of our **sophisms** <note>original: "sophismatis oder verführischen Reden." Alchemical writers often used "sophisms"—intentionally deceptive or riddling language—to hide their secrets from those they deemed unworthy.</note>, namely, that in our true **Magical Fire** there is no **sublimation** <term>Sublimation: In chemistry, the transition of a substance from solid to gas. In alchemy, it often refers to the spiritualization or "uplifting" of matter, but here the author argues the fire itself does not move upward because it is already omnipresent.</term>, for the fire is the life, and the life is the soul, which is not subject to the **dimensions** <note>original: "Dimensionibus."</note> of the body. It is not the substance or the essence, or the matter of any thing in the **glass** <note>Referring to the alchemical flask or vessel.</note>, or of that which is **volatile** <note>original: "flüchtig."</note> or **fixed** <note>original: "fix." "Fixed" substances are those that can withstand the heat of the fire without evaporating.</note>. That is to say, this fire may be extinguished or killed, and yet the substance is not diminished in the slightest, as is plainly to be seen in the loss of those whose fire goes out during the **process** of this work; for the cooling of the matter immediately ruins and kills the entire **composite** <note>original: "Compositum." The mixture of substances currently undergoing transformation in the vessel.</note>, according to the teaching of **Flamel** <note>Nicolas Flamel (c. 1330–1418), a famous French figure often associated with the discovery of the Philosopher’s Stone.</note> and others. That is, it makes the matter unfit for any future **movement** <note>original: "Bewegung." In this context, movement refers to the internal "life" or chemical activity required for the Stone to mature.</note>.

<summary>The author explains that the "Magical Fire" is a spiritual force akin to the soul, existing everywhere at once within the alchemical vessel. He warns that if this fire is lost and the matter cools, the entire "composite" dies and the process cannot be resumed.</summary>

<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Emerald Tablet, soul and body, Magical Fire, sublimation, fixed and volatile, Nicolas Flamel, vital heat</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Magical Fire" mentioned on the previous page. It moves from describing the fire’s omnipresence to defining its nature as a spiritual "Sulfur" and a "fermenting scent" that originates from invisible realms, citing influential alchemists like Van Helmont and Sendivogius.</meta>

<page-num>60</page-num>

movement toward the perfection of the **Magisterium** <term>Magisterium: The completed alchemical work or the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</term>, which is the symptom or accident <note>original: "Zufall." In a philosophical sense, an "accident" is a property that a thing possesses but which is not essential to its nature.</note> of the inner heat that works within here; although in this clarification <note>original: "Erklärung." This could also refer to the dissolution or "opening" of the matter.</note> of the **matter**, not a single grain of the substance perishes, yet the life is irretrievably lost.

Thus, the **fire** in the work—which we call "our fire" for the sake of distinction—is this hidden inner **Sulfur**. It is not found in its weight and substance, but rather in its heavenly, spiritual power, which is nothing other than the invisible heaven in this subterranean **compound** <note>original: "Composito."</note>. This is what the noble **Helmont** <note>Jan Baptista van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneering chemist who believed "ferments" or spiritual seeds directed the growth of all matter.</note> calls his **fermental odor** <note>original: "odorem fermentalem."</note> or fermenting scent, which scent brings forth actual, visible, and bodily effects <note>original: "effectus."</note>. Therefore, those are certainly on the side of truth who hold and teach that the world and all things within it have their origin from invisible things <note>original: "ab invisibilibus."</note>. This is the **Central Fire** of **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a Polish alchemist who wrote about a "central fire" at the heart of the earth that drives the growth of minerals.</note>, and the invisible mover <note>original: "invisibilis motor."</note> of the ancients, which is uniform in all things, yet distinguished within them through the effects by their kinds and
& <note>This catchword indicates the next page begins with a word such as "genera" or "species."</note>

<summary>This page defines the alchemical "fire" as a spiritual, invisible sulfur or ferment that originates from a heavenly source to produce physical effects in matter. It draws on the theories of Van Helmont and Sendivogius to argue that all physical things originate from invisible, spiritual causes.</summary>
<keywords>Magisterium, internal heat, Sulfur, Van Helmont, fermental odor, Sendivogius, Central Fire, invisible mover, spiritual matter</keywords>

--- Page 353 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "secret fire" or "metallic life" from the previous page, describing it as an autonomous force within the matter that works toward a state of rest.</meta>

<page-num>61</page-num>

& species <note>original: "species." In alchemy, this refers to the specific form or essence of a substance.</note>. It appears like an internal **intelligence** that carries out its duty according to whether the external governance has been observed or violated; and it never ceases—unless it is interrupted or prevented—until the end is reached, in which it rests and keeps its **Sabbath** <note>The "Sabbath" is a common alchemical metaphor for the completion of the "Great Work," when the matter has reached its most perfect, stable state and no longer requires the labor of the chemist.</note>.

Therefore, when we speak of our **fire**, which **sublimates** <term>Sublimation: The process of heating a substance until it becomes a vapor and then solidifies again, used here to describe the spiritual refinement of the matter.</term>, remember what is to be understood by this and in what sense or meaning; nevertheless, you must nonetheless **circulate** the moisture of the **compound** <note>original: "Compositi."</note>, even if during the time of this true circulation the **metallic life**—which is our **secret fire**—is not strongly moved, but is the same above and below and in all places. 

And to bring this conclusion to a full close, let me report and remind you that you should look well to the **matter** which is the **subject** <note>original: "subjectum."</note> of this present conclusion, and which we have indicated and described. Take a pure **seed** and a pure **matrix** or **womb** <note>original: "matricem oder Bährmutter." Alchemists often used biological metaphors; the "seed" is the active sulfurous principle, and the "matrix" is the receptive mercurial vessel or substance.</note>; see that you have first well devised and found it before you set it in (for from a raven no other [bird is born] <note>This is a common alchemical maxim: "Ex corvo non nascitur nisi corvus." It implies that the quality of the result depends entirely on the nature of the starting material.</note>

<summary>This page describes the "secret fire" as an autonomous internal intelligence that directs the alchemical process toward its final completion, or "Sabbath." It emphasizes that the chemist must provide a pure "seed" and "womb" (starting materials) for this metallic life to manifest properly.</summary>

<keywords>internal intelligence, alchemical Sabbath, sublimation, circulation, metallic life, secret fire, matter, seed, matrix, womb</keywords>

--- Page 354 ---
<meta>This page concludes the instructions regarding the "secret fire" and the "subject" (the matter) mentioned on page 61. It describes the final stages of the alchemical process. Note: The source indicates water staining on the right side of the original page.</meta>

<page-num>62</page-num>

...should it come as nothing other than a **Raven’s Egg** <note>original: "Raben-Ey." In alchemy, the "egg" refers to the matter contained within the hermetically sealed vessel, while the "raven" symbolizes the initial black stage of decay and purification.</note>, then you shall obtain a noble and pure fruit. Let your external fire be of such a nature that your **preparations** <note>original: "Confectiones." These are the refined chemical mixtures or medicinal compounds being processed.</note> can be moved back and forth and **circulate** <term>Circulation: The continuous process of evaporation and condensation within a sealed vessel, intended to "spiritually" refine the substance.</term> without ceasing; and in a few weeks, this will bring forth what you most desire: first the **Raven’s Beak** <note>original: "Raben-Schnabel." A reference to the black stage (*Nigredo*), often signifying the appearance of a dark precipitate or "beak" emerging from the matter.</note>, which will afterwards transform into a **White Dove** <note>original: "weisse Taube." This represents the *Albedo* or whitening stage, signifying that the matter has been purified and is reaching a higher state of stability.</note>, and finally into a **Tyrian Purple color** <note>original: "Tyrische Purpur-Farbe." This represents the final stage of the Great Work, known as *Rubedo* or the reddening, which indicates the successful creation of the Philosopher's Stone or the highest medicinal essence.</note>.

->To God Alone be the Glory<- <note>original: "S. D. S. G.," an abbreviation for the Latin *Soli Deo Sit Gloria*.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the technical instructions for the alchemical process, guiding the reader through the symbolic color changes—from the black "Raven" to the "White Dove" and finally the "Tyrian Purple"—that signal the completion of the Work.</summary>
